# Coverage Audit Report

- **Epic / workflow:** `effect-provider-epic`
- **Scope source:** `specs/desired_program_model/ticket_plan.yaml` (lines 538-663)
- **Models audited:** each example project's listed `Core.tla`, `Internal.tla`,
  `External.tla`, CFG, manifest, action/binding files, adapter declarations, and
  projection at commit `20f0cb70058e8e514e4df3b1af09a40df97dffa5`
- **Date:** `2026-07-22`
- **Verdict:** `FAIL`

> This audit checks completeness of what is modeled, not fidelity. The four
> conformance/mutation oracles are bounded to generated cases and modeled
> boundaries. All three experiment campaigns can remain green while the gaps
> below remain absent from the model.

---

## 0. Declared scope (quoted verbatim from the plan)

The workflow has no in-flight ticket (`active_ticket: null`, line 533). The
owner therefore supplied a workflow-level exact-path scope. The following is
literal text from the cited line range, without paraphrase.

```yaml
# specs/desired_program_model/ticket_plan.yaml:538-663
service_catalog:
  existing_boundaries:
    - tla-spec-dev CLI (scaffold/open/run/close)
    - scripts/generate_cases_from_tlc_dump.py case generation
    - scripts/run_generated_case_adapters.py adapter runner
    - spec_manifest.yaml / actions.yml / testgraph_bindings.yml schemas
  desired_boundaries:
    - budgets block in spec_manifest.yaml read by all gates
    - analyze complexity pre-TLC gate
    - sandboxed effect observation in the adapter runner
    - channel-authentic Test Graph bindings with double|real port configurations
    - typed semantic effect ports selected per generated action
    - project-owned providers bound around batch case lifecycle
    - deterministic seed/replay metadata for bounded fuzz iterations
  adapter_boundaries:
    - specs/program_model/production_adapters.py spec-unit adapters
    - test_graph specWorkflow / cliWorkflow graphs
  known_gaps:
    - "The inherited host model does not model provider configuration or fuzz iteration as CLI state. EP-01 and EP-02 therefore record zero host-TLA deltas unless implementation exposes new modeled CLI behavior; EP-03 supplies independent TLA+ models in the example projects."
    - "A provider can install a monkeypatch or supply an explicitly injected port, but V0 does not prove that application code cannot bypass it. The existing passive effect observer remains the separate leak-detection mechanism."

coverage_audit_scope:
  revision: 1
  declaration: >-
    Audit the complete non-mutant runtime behavior of exactly the seven Python
    application and entrypoint files below against each project's own Internal
    and External TLA+ views. Exact-path matching applies: a listed file does not
    imply directory closure.
  in_scope_application_files:
    - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/application.py
    - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/atomic_cli.py
    - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/legacy_payment_http_app/__init__.py
    - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/legacy_payment_http_app/__main__.py
    - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/legacy_payment_http_app/application.py
    - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/app.py
    - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/reminder_cli.py
  model_indexes:
    atomic_publisher:
      application_files:
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/application.py
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/atomic_cli.py
      model_and_binding_files:
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/Core.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/Internal.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/Internal.cfg
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/External.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/External.cfg
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/spec_manifest.yaml
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/actions.yml
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/case_adapters.toml
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/testgraph_bindings.yml
        - examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/tlc_projection.py
    legacy_payment_http:
      application_files:
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/legacy_payment_http_app/__init__.py
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/legacy_payment_http_app/__main__.py
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/legacy_payment_http_app/application.py
      model_and_binding_files:
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/Core.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/Internal.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/Internal.cfg
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/External.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/External.cfg
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/spec_manifest.yaml
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/actions.yml
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/case_adapters.toml
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/testgraph_bindings.yml
        - examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/tlc_projection.py
    reminder_worker:
      application_files:
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/app.py
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/reminder_cli.py
      model_and_binding_files:
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/Core.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/Internal.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/Internal.cfg
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/External.tla
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/External.cfg
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/spec_manifest.yaml
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/actions.yml
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/case_adapters.toml
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/testgraph_bindings.yml
        - examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/tlc_projection.py
  included_behavior:
    - >-
      Every control-path success, modeled failure, exception normalization,
      retry, order, cardinality, serialization, persistence, CLI ingress, CLI
      egress, and effect attempt implemented by an in-scope file is included.
    - >-
      A behavior is not excluded merely because the fixed generated corpus does
      not exercise it or because a provider, adapter, shared journal, baseline,
      passive guard, or hand-written assertion detects it.
    - >-
      Representation requires positive evidence in a named TLA+ action, state,
      invariant, or explicit refinement across both views. Provider-owned or
      test-owned checks are validation evidence, not model coverage.
  explicit_behavior_exclusions:
    - >-
      Atomic-publisher fault-injection selection (`MUTANTS`,
      `ATOMIC_PUBLISHER_MUTANT`, `_selected_mutant`) and branch arms reachable
      only when a non-null AP-* mutant is selected are test mutations, not
      production control behavior.
    - >-
      Legacy-payment fault-injection selection (`LEGACY_PAYMENT_MUTANT`,
      `_mutant`) and branch arms reachable only when the value is not CONTROL
      are test mutations, not production control behavior.
    - >-
      Reminder-worker branch arms reachable only when `run_once(mutant=...)`
      receives an RW-* identifier are test mutations, not production control
      behavior; the default `mutant=None` path remains fully in scope.
  out_of_scope_rule: >-
    Every tracked application or implementation source path not listed in
    in_scope_application_files is out of this application coverage audit. This
    is an exact-path exclusion, not an instruction to omit those rows from the
    mechanical repository inventory. The model_and_binding_files are the
    mandatory representation index, not application-surface rows.
  out_of_scope_categories:
    - project providers, adapters, conformance helpers, baselines, probes, tests, regeneration, experiment runners, and mutation scorers
    - generated contracts and cases, raw evidence, reports, documentation, and Test Graph control-plane code
    - host EP-01/EP-02 framework runtime and CLI code, whose zero host-model delta has separate unit, spec, TLC, and graph evidence
    - all Java, Kotlin, shell, and other tracked program source because Python V0 is the declared experiment boundary
  requirements:
    - mechanically inventory every tracked program-source file before applying the exact-path classification
    - build a separate non-empty representation index for each project's Internal and External views
    - audit both views and all effect categories; do not pool one project's representation into another
    - report every in-scope partial or unrepresented behavior as a hard gap; do not repair it during the audit
```

| Scope line | Covers |
|---|---|
| `ticket_plan.yaml:559-565` | Complete non-mutant runtime behavior of exactly seven Python application/entrypoint files; file names do not imply directory closure. |
| `ticket_plan.yaml:566-573` | The seven exact in-scope application paths. |
| `ticket_plan.yaml:574-620` | Separate mandatory model/binding indexes for atomic publisher, legacy HTTP, and reminder worker. |
| `ticket_plan.yaml:621-633` | Successes, failures, retries, order/cardinality, serialization, persistence, CLI ingress/egress, and effect attempts; provider/test checks are not model coverage. |
| `ticket_plan.yaml:634-647` | Only non-control AP/PH/RW mutation-selection branches are excluded. |
| `ticket_plan.yaml:648-658` | Every other tracked application/implementation source path is exactly out of scope, but must remain in the mechanical inventory. |
| `ticket_plan.yaml:659-663` | Inventory all program source, build six non-empty view indexes, audit all effect categories, and treat every scoped partial/unrepresented behavior as hard. |

**Escalations:** none. Every mechanically enumerated source path traces either
to the seven-file list or to the exact-path out-of-scope rule. Every in-scope
behavior traces to the included/excluded behavior lines.

## 1. Model representation index

The required parameterized-action regex was run separately for all six view
modules. The full 299-line command output, including Core/helper operators,
CFGs, ports,
action declarations, effect-port declarations, and external bindings, is
preserved in
[`model-representation-index.txt`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/model-representation-index.txt).
All six transition indexes contain seven actions and are plausible against the
seven scenario/outcome catalogs.

| Kind | Project / view | Names | `file:line` |
|---|---|---|---|
| Actions | Atomic / Internal | `CreateSuccess`, `ValidUpdate`, `IdempotentRetry`, `StaleRevision`, `ReadFailure`, `StagedWriteFailure`, `ReplaceFailure` | `examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/Internal.tla:25-31` |
| Actions | Atomic / External | `InvokeCreateSuccess`, `InvokeValidUpdate`, `InvokeIdempotentRetry`, `InvokeStaleRevision`, `InvokeReadFailure`, `InvokeStagedWriteFailure`, `InvokeReplaceFailure` | `examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/External.tla:15-21` |
| Port | Atomic | `FilesystemPort` (`read`, `write`, `replace`, `delete`) | `examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/spec_manifest.yaml:56-71` |
| Bindings | Atomic | all 14 actions bind `FilesystemPort`; seven external actions use `filesystem` | `examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/actions.yml:2-71`; `examples/effect_providers/atomic_publisher/specs/program_model/testgraph_bindings.yml:6-76` |
| Actions | HTTP / Internal | seven `Authorize*` outcomes | `examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/Internal.tla:23-136` |
| Actions | HTTP / External | seven `Submit*` outcomes | `examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/External.tla:21-51` |
| Port | HTTP | `PaymentHttpPort.send(PaymentHttpRequest)` | `examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/spec_manifest.yaml:27-33` |
| Bindings | HTTP | Internal actions bind `PaymentHttpPort`; External actions declare no effect port and use an `http` channel; external config also names `ProcessPort` | `examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/actions.yml:2-71`; `examples/effect_providers/legacy_payment_http/specs/program_model/testgraph_bindings.yml:1-56` |
| Actions | Reminder / Internal | seven `Process*` scenarios | `examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/Internal.tla:29-104` |
| Actions | Reminder / External | seven `Run*` scenarios | `examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/External.tla:29-104` |
| Ports | Reminder | `ClockPort`, `QueuePort`, `OutboxPort`, `NotifierPort` | `examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/spec_manifest.yaml:77-116` |
| Bindings | Reminder | Internal actions list all four ports; External actions list none and use a `process` channel | `examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/actions.yml:2-71`; `examples/effect_providers/reminder_worker/specs/program_model/testgraph_bindings.yml:1-57` |

Positive representation is narrow: every view is a one-step terminal-outcome
model. A port declaration or provider assertion does not manufacture missing
TLA+ order, request, response, CLI, persistence, or exception semantics.

## 2. Sweep 1 — Program surface

### Enumeration

No test, vendored, history, generated, or evidence filter was applied: such a
filter would silently remove tracked source rows that the exact-path rule says
must remain inventoried.

```bash
git ls-files '*.py' | sort > /tmp/ca-surface-py.txt
git ls-files '*.java' | sort > /tmp/ca-surface-java.txt
git ls-files '*.kt' | sort > /tmp/ca-surface-kt.txt
git ls-files '*.kts' | sort > /tmp/ca-surface-kts.txt
git ls-files '*.sh' | sort > /tmp/ca-surface-sh.txt
git ls-files '*.j2' | sort > /tmp/ca-surface-j2.txt
git ls-files --stage | awk '$1 == "100755" {file=$4; n=split(file, parts, "/"); if (parts[n] !~ /\./) print file}' | sort > /tmp/ca-surface-executable-noext.txt
git ls-files '*Dockerfile*' | sort > /tmp/ca-surface-dockerfile.txt
cat /tmp/ca-surface-py.txt /tmp/ca-surface-java.txt /tmp/ca-surface-kt.txt /tmp/ca-surface-kts.txt /tmp/ca-surface-sh.txt /tmp/ca-surface-j2.txt /tmp/ca-surface-executable-noext.txt /tmp/ca-surface-dockerfile.txt | sort -u > /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt
```

| Language/source class | Recorded command | N |
|---|---|---:|
| Python | `git ls-files '*.py' \| sort > /tmp/ca-surface-py.txt` | 2,092 |
| Java | `git ls-files '*.java' \| sort > /tmp/ca-surface-java.txt` | 264 |
| Kotlin | `git ls-files '*.kt' \| sort > /tmp/ca-surface-kt.txt` | 462 |
| Kotlin script | `git ls-files '*.kts' \| sort > /tmp/ca-surface-kts.txt` | 110 |
| Shell | `git ls-files '*.sh' \| sort > /tmp/ca-surface-sh.txt` | 5 |
| Jinja source templates | `git ls-files '*.j2' \| sort > /tmp/ca-surface-j2.txt` | 12 |
| Executable, no extension | `git ls-files --stage \| awk '$1 == "100755" { ... basename has no dot ... }' \| sort` | 62 |
| Dockerfile | `git ls-files '*Dockerfile*' \| sort` | 1 |
| Unique union | `cat /tmp/ca-surface-*.txt \| sort -u > /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt` | **3,008** |

### Sweep-1 result

All seven exact in-scope modules are either `partial` (six) or
`unrepresented` (the HTTP package-export module). The remaining 3,001 rows are
`unrepresented` against these three project indexes and inventoried, not gated,
under the quoted exact-path exclusion.

---

## 3. Sweep 2 — Effects, by category

Every grep ran against the exact 3,008-path union via
`xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '<pattern>' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt`; timeout matching
used `grep -HniE`. Patterns were extended for JVM APIs and the scoped projects'
semantic port calls (`self._filesystem.*`, `session.send`, queue/outbox methods,
notification sends, and CLI parsing/stdio). No category was searched in a
hardcoded subdirectory.

```bash
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(open|Path|write_text|read_text|write_bytes|mkdir|makedirs|remove|unlink|rename|replace|copy|copytree|rmtree|tempfile|mkdtemp|NamedTemporaryFile|File|Files|FileInputStream|FileOutputStream|readText|writeText|createDirectories|deleteIfExists|move)\b|self\._filesystem\.(read|write|replace|delete)' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-filesystem.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(subprocess|Popen|run|call|check_output|check_call|system|execv|execve|spawn|ProcessBuilder|Runtime\.getRuntime|exec)\b' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-subprocess.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(socket|connect|requests|urlopen|urlretrieve|urllib|httpx|aiohttp|HTTPConnection|curl|wget|HttpClient|HttpURLConnection|URI|URL)\b|session\.send|Session\(' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-network.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(environ|getenv|putenv|setdefault|argv|load_dotenv|expanduser|PATH|System\.getenv|System\.getProperty|System\.setProperty)\b|parse_args\(' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-environment.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(datetime|now|utcnow|today|time|monotonic|perf_counter|sleep|timestamp|System\.currentTimeMillis|System\.nanoTime|Instant\.now|LocalDateTime\.now|Thread\.sleep)\b' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-clock.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(random|randint|choice|shuffle|sample|uuid|uuid4|secrets|urandom|token_hex|Random|UUID\.randomUUID|SecureRandom)\b' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-randomness.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(sqlite3|psycopg|pymysql|redis|boto3|engine|session|cursor|execute|commit|DataSource|DriverManager|getConnection|EntityManager|JdbcTemplate|queue|outbox|claim|acknowledge|release|dead_letter|lookup|stage|mark_sent)\b' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-persistent_store.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(notifier|notify|notification|smtp|kafka|publish|send_message)\b|\.send\(' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-notification.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(print|input|stdout|stderr|System\.out|System\.err|System\.in|println|printf)\b' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-effect-stdio.txt
```

The raw output and a formal semantic TSV are retained per category. The raw
file is the full lexical accounting, including every destructive site; there is
no sampling or high-volume grouping. The formal TSV omits only scoped lines
read and classified as lexical false
positives, and its verdict column contains only `declared`, `undeclared`, or
`partial`. `READ` means the whole in-scope file was read. `INFERRED` means only
exact-path scope was dispositioned and source semantics were deliberately not
claimed.

| Category | Raw accounting / formal table | Raw N | Formal M | Scoped READ / out-of-scope INFERRED | Scoped false-positive collapse |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Filesystem | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-filesystem.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-filesystem-table.tsv) | 22,818 | 22,810 | 21 / 22,797 | 8 |
| Subprocess | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-subprocess.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-subprocess-table.tsv) | 7,640 | 7,640 | 0 / 7,640 | 0 |
| Network | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-network.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-network-table.tsv) | 180 | 173 | 11 / 169 | 7 |
| Environment / CLI ingress | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-environment.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-environment-table.tsv) | 1,935 | 1,935 | 5 / 1,930 | 0 |
| Clock | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-clock.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-clock-table.tsv) | 1,363 | 1,362 | 5 / 1,358 | 1 |
| Randomness | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-randomness.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-randomness-table.tsv) | 72 | 72 | 0 / 72 | 0 |
| Persistent store | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-persistent_store.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-persistent_store-table.tsv) | 1,096 | 1,062 | 55 / 1,041 | 34 |
| Notification/messaging | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-notification.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-notification-table.tsv) | 416 | 409 | 10 / 406 | 7 |
| Standard I/O | [`raw`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-stdio.txt) / [`formal`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/effect-stdio-table.tsv) | 9,967 | 9,967 | 1 / 9,966 | 0 |

Collapse rule: only a line in one of the seven files was collapsed, and only
after reading it and determining it was an import/type annotation/docstring,
non-effect state expression/definition/string reference, cross-category match,
or lexical accident. Actual reminder CLI journal calls remain scoped effects. All
out-of-scope lexical hits remain row-for-row in the TSV rather than being
silently called false positives. Destructive calls remain per-site.

No scoped file contains a spawn site. The external adapters' spawn sites are
out of scope by lines 648-658. Their process channel cannot declare effects
performed by the child: the scoped child filesystem/stdout/persistence effects
above remain undeclared or partial.

## 4. Sweep 3 — Behaviors

The prompt's sample commands hardcode `scripts/ spec_double_compiler/`, which
would yield **zero scoped rows** here. To honor its earlier rule that every
later sweep derives from Sweep 1, every behavior pattern instead ran through
`xargs ... < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt`. The exact commands/patterns and full
per-hit tables are represented by the raw/table pairs below. Every raw row is
accounted; no high-volume category was sampled.

```bash
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE 'except|raise|try:' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-behavior-errors.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(retry|backoff|attempt|max_tries|max_attempts|continue)\b' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-behavior-retries.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HniE '\b(timeout|deadline|expires|TimeoutError|ConnectTimeout|ReadTimeout|REQUEST_TIMEOUT)\b' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-behavior-timeouts.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(fallback|default|ImportError)\b|or None|except[^#]*pass|\.get\([^)]*,| if .* else ' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-behavior-fallbacks.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE '\b(thread|async|await|lock|Lock|concurrent|multiprocessing|synchronized|Thread|Executor|Coroutine|mutex)\b' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-behavior-concurrency.txt
xargs -n 40 grep -HnE 'if[^#]*(config|getenv)|\bgetenv\b|\.get\(|\b(flag|enabled|scenario|max_attempts|base_url)\b|--no-|--allow|parse_args|argparse' < /tmp/ca-surface-all.txt > /tmp/ca-behavior-config.txt
```

| Project / surface item | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Atomic publication | `partial` | Seven terminal actions/traces at `Internal.tla:25-31` positively cover ordinary terminal classes; `application.py:23-39,46-123,133-142` adds request/prestate guards, byte serialization, malformed records, false effect results, residue, and cleanup failures absent from state/actions. |
| HTTP authorization | `partial` | Seven terminal actions at `Internal.tla:23-136` retain only outcome/result/attempt count; `application.py:38-126` implements request construction, response classification, retry steps, timeout and exceptional semantics not represented. |
| Reminder processing | `partial` | Seven terminal actions at `Internal.tla:29-104` retain positive final queue/outbox/count/receipt/result projections; `app.py:54-121` implements ordered/correlated calls, due comparison, command fields, failure propagation, and supported pending-outbox × retryable/permanent outcomes not represented. |

### 5.2 External — verdict: `FAIL`

| Project / surface item | Verdict | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| Atomic CLI | `partial` | `External.tla:15-21` wraps the same terminal actions; `atomic_cli.py:11-38` exposes argv, filesystem result/stage observation, JSON and exit behavior absent from the view. |
| HTTP package + CLI | `partial` | `External.tla:21-51` exposes payment/amount/key/outcome but omits public export shape, base URL, argv conversions/errors, stdout JSON, exit, per-request effects, and response mapping in `__init__.py:1-5`, `__main__.py:9-27`, `application.py:38-126`. |
| Reminder process/CLI | `partial` | `External.tla:29-104` duplicates terminal projection; `reminder_cli.py:30-204` adds scenario/config, per-event and post-mutation flushes with observable intermediate persisted states, journal/order, assertion/failure, serialization and result-file effects absent from the view. |

Both views exist for all three projects; none is missing. Keeping the view
verdicts separate exposes that Internal lacks protocol detail while External
additionally lacks the actual entrypoint and observable I/O contracts.

## 6. Dispositions

### 6.0 Mechanical mapping from formal rows to dispositions

Every in-scope formal row with a gap verdict maps to exactly one consolidated
gap ID; each in-scope Sweep-1 module row maps to one or more IDs because a file
can contain independent gaps. `declared`/`represented` rows require no
disposition, and exact out-of-scope or mutant-excluded rows are inventory only.
The complete 124-row relation is
[`gap-disposition-map.tsv`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/gap-disposition-map.tsv).
The retained dependency-free
[`verify_tables.py`](coverage-audit-sweep-raw/verify_tables.py) recomputes this
relation and refuses if an in-scope `partial`/`undeclared` effect row or
`partial`/`unrepresented` behavior/module row lacks an ID. It verifies row
accounting/vocabularies/mapping; it does not pretend to infer the semantic
classifications, which remain the auditor's recorded judgment.

| Gap ID | Formal row mappings |
|---|---:|
| `CA-01` | 1 |
| `CA-02` | 10 |
| `CA-03` | 10 |
| `CA-04` | 4 |
| `CH-01` | 9 |
| `CH-02` | 9 |
| `CH-03` | 18 |
| `CH-04` | 14 |
| `CR-01` | 1 |
| `CR-02` | 6 |
| `CR-03` | 39 |
| `CR-04` | 3 |

This consolidation grants positive credit to the terminal projections that do
exist: the IDs name only the additional relation/effect/behavior missing from
those partial rows.

### 6.1 In-scope gaps — HARD, block promotion

| # | Gap | Sweep | Disposition | Proposed remediation (advisory) |
|---:|---|---|---|---|
| CA-01 | Atomic canonical UTF-8/sorted/compact byte serialization and exact request-field preservation are test/provider assertions, not TLA state or invariants. | 1, 4, views | **Model it** | Add semantic command fields plus a serialization obligation/refinement; keep concrete bytes provider-owned but make preservation/canonicalization a modeled obligation. |
| CA-02 | Atomic malformed-record, partial staged-write residue, ignored normal false `written`/`replaced`/`deleted` results, and cleanup-delete failure behavior has no scenario/state/outcome. | 1, errors, views | **Model it** | Add finite read-decode/validation, effect-result, and cleanup-result classes plus stage-file state; alternatively normalize/remove those branches, then model the smaller contract. |
| CA-03 | Atomic CLI argv, result-file write, stage-existence observation, JSON/exit/failure behavior and direct FS effects are absent. | effects, config, External | **Model it** | Add explicit CLI input/output/effect actions and a filesystem/result projection, including parse/write failures. |
| CA-04 | Atomic has no modeled/refined relation from `PublishRequest` plus existing-record/prestate to scenario: id/payload/revision guards, no-record with arbitrary expected revision, and final/stage path relationships are selected outside TLA. | 1, fallbacks/config, Internal | **Model it** | Add finite request/prestate variables and guard/refinement mapping into the seven positively retained terminal classes/traces. |
| CH-01 | HTTP public package export and CLI base-url/argv/stdout/exit contract is absent from External. | 1, effects, External | **Model it** | Add an entrypoint action/state projection for CLI inputs, stdout result and process status; include the public function surface as the external contract. |
| CH-02 | HTTP request protocol (POST/path/body/payment id/headers/stable idempotency key/session policy/timeout) is encoded in provider assertions, not TLA; `PaymentHttpRequest` is incomplete. | network, views | **Model it** | Add a normalized semantic request/effect plan with required command fields and request obligations; do not encode concrete byte scripts. |
| CH-03 | HTTP retries are terminal attempt counts only; per-attempt response class, ordering, stable key and arbitrary `max_attempts` behavior (including <=0 and >3) are absent. | retries, config, Internal | **Model it** | Split attempts into transitions and make the supported attempt-limit domain explicit. If only 3 is intended, change the program API to enforce that boundary and model the refusal. |
| CH-04 | HTTP response normalization omits `unexpected_response`, arbitrary/default reasons, reference validity/preservation, status/body mapping, and unhandled request exceptions. | errors, fallbacks, views | **Model it** | Model finite response classes and normalized results/errors, including unexpected-valid and exceptional transport classes; retain opaque concrete values behind equality/class obligations. |
| CR-01 | Reminder effect order/cardinality, command data, duplicate-send prohibition, receipt preservation and stage-before-send behavior are provider/journal assertions, not TLA transitions/invariants. | persistent, notification, Internal | **Model it** | Generate a semantic effect plan: ordered effect phases, per-port cardinality, required command fields, response class and projection obligations. |
| CR-02 | Reminder due comparison (`now < due_at`, equality is due) and non-notifier port/unknown notifier failure propagation are absent. | clock, errors, Internal | **Model it** | Add finite clock/due relation and effect-failure classes, or normalize/remove unsupported propagation in the program and model the changed behavior. |
| CR-03 | Reminder CLI accepts unknown scenarios and implements unmodeled per-event plus post-mutation file flushes (exposing intermediate journal/queue/outbox states), assertions, invalid receipt projection, result serialization, argv and file/process failures. | filesystem, config, errors, External | **Change the program** | Reject unknown scenarios at ingress and move file-backed implementations behind explicit ports; then model remaining CLI inputs/outputs, persistence transitions/effects and normalized failures. |
| CR-04 | The scenario catalog correlates initial outbox state with notifier outcome. The program supports cross-products absent from both views, including pending outbox followed by retryable or permanent notification failure. | errors, Internal, External | **Model it** | Factor initial queue/outbox state and notifier response class into independent finite dimensions, then model reachable cross-products and their terminal/intermediate projections. |

## 7. Verdict

- In-scope hard gaps: **12**
- In-scope modules with complete representation: **0 / 7**
- Out-of-scope program-source rows inventoried: **3,001**
- Scope escalations: **0**
- **Verdict: `FAIL`**

Promotion is blocked until each hard gap is closed by modeling it or changing
the program. This verdict does not retract the experiment's 36/36 fixed-catalog
mutation result: it explains why that result is bounded. Twenty of those kills
were already attributed to provider/shared-journal assertions; this audit
confirms that core order, request, response, command and CLI intuition remains
outside TLA+.

## 8. Attestation

1. **Row-count reconciliation.** Sweep 1: Python 2,092 + Java 264 + Kotlin 462
   + KTS 110 + shell 5 + Jinja source templates 12 + executable/no-extension
   62 + Dockerfile 1 = 3,008 unique, table 3,008, equal. Sweep 2 raw/formal/
   collapsed triples: filesystem 22,818/22,810/8; subprocess 7,640/7,640/0;
   network 180/173/7; environment 1,935/1,935/0; clock 1,363/1,362/1;
   randomness 72/72/0; persistent store 1,096/1,062/34; notification
   416/409/7; stdio 9,967/9,967/0. Sweep 3: errors 3,031/3,030/1;
   retries 873/873/0; timeouts 1,276/1,276/0; fallbacks 4,216/4,216/0;
   concurrency 99/99/0; config 4,499/4,499/0. In every triple,
   `raw N = formal M + explicitly enumerated collapse`. Six view indexes exist
   and each has seven transition actions. All equalities can be recounted from
   the retained raw/table files and `verify_tables.py`.
2. **Surface NOT walked.** No in-scope application file, listed model/binding
   file, effect category, behavior class, or view was omitted. The semantics of
   the 3,001 exact-path out-of-scope source files were not read; their paths and
   lexical hits were inventoried only. Declarative/docs/binary formats outside
   the listed model indexes (`json`, generic YAML/TOML/properties/TLA, reports,
   archives, images) were not treated as runtime program-source rows; the plan
   explicitly places them out of this application audit. This is an explicit
   boundary, not a claim that they contain no behavior.
3. **READ versus INFERRED.** Program surface: 7 READ, 3,001 INFERRED. Effects:
   clock 5/1,358; environment 5/1,930; filesystem 21/22,797; network 11/169;
   notification 10/406; persistent store 55/1,041; randomness 0/72; stdio
   1/9,966; subprocess 0/7,640. Behaviors: concurrency 0/99; config 35/4,464;
   errors 28/3,003; fallbacks 17/4,199; retries 15/858; timeouts 3/1,273.
   Views and their mandatory indexes: all listed files READ, 0 INFERRED. The
   inferred rows are exactly those marked `INFERRED exact-path scope; source
   not read` in TSVs; listing thousands again here would duplicate the
   mechanically complete artifacts.
4. **Scope by reasoning.** None. Every source row cites either lines 566-573 or
   648-658; every behavior exclusion cites 634-647. There are zero escalations.
5. **Reproducibility.** Yes. The raw source inventories, raw grep outputs,
   exact counts, model index, and generated per-hit TSVs are retained. A reader
   can reproduce every row set without trusting the prose summary.
6. **Findings about this prompt.** Three weaknesses remain visible:

   - Step 4's literal sample commands search only `scripts/` and
     `spec_double_compiler/`, contradicting the earlier rule that all later
     sweeps use Sweep 1. Following them literally would have produced zero
     scoped behavior rows and a plausible false report. This run used the full
     surface and records the deviation.
   - Even word-bounded lexical patterns generate extreme noise in an
     append-only repository (`run`, `Path`, `print`, copied history/evidence).
     A 62,489-data-row accounting (3,008 surface + 45,487 effect + 13,994
     behavior rows) can prove no row was silently dropped while still
     leaving 59,000+ rows semantically inferred. The attestation, not the
     flattering equality, is load-bearing.
   - “Adapt the glob to the project's languages” still lets the auditor choose
     what counts as program source. This run included Python, JVM sources,
     shell, source templates, executable extensionless scripts and Dockerfile,
     and explicitly names excluded declarative formats. A mechanical inventory
     with repository-owned source-kind policy is still needed; self-generated
     `N == M` remains an honesty check, not an independent guarantee.
